The proof will be divided into two parts.
First, we will show that for anyH,G∈ℋ​(W,λ)H,G\in\mathcal{H}(W,\lambda), we havedSBM​(U​(H),U​(G))≤dCBM​(αH,αG).d_{\rm SBM}({\rm U}(H),{\rm U}(G))\leq d_{\rm CBM}(\alpha_{H},\alpha_{G}).

Recall some notations from[36]. Let(M,ξ=ker⁡α0)(M,\xi=\ker\alpha_{0})be a closed and Liouville-fillable. Consider the associated Liouville domain(W,λ,L)(W,\lambda,L)such that∂W=M\partial W=M,λ|M=α0\lambda|_{M}=\alpha_{0}and the flow ofLLis complete fort<0t<0. Letα∈O(M,ξ)​(α0)\alpha\in O_{(M,\xi)}(\alpha_{0}), thenα=ef​α0\alpha=e^{f}\alpha_{0}for somef:M→ℝf\colon M\rightarrow\mathbb{R}. DefineWα≔{(s,x)∈W^∣s≤ef​(x)}.W^{\alpha}\coloneqq\{(s,x)\in\hat{W}\mid s\leq e^{f(x)}\}.By Lemma 8 in[36], for anyα1,α2∈O(M,ξ)​(α0)\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in O_{(M,\xi)}(\alpha_{0}), we have(28)dSBM​(Wα1,Wα2)≤dCBM​(α1,α2).d_{\rm SBM}(W^{\alpha_{1}},W^{\alpha_{2}})\leq d_{\rm CBM}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}).

Take(M,α0)=(∂WH,αH)(M,\alpha_{0})=(\partial W_{H},\alpha_{H})and(W,λ,L)=(WH,λ^,Y^)(W,\lambda,L)=(W_{H},\hat{\lambda},\widehat{Y}), whereY^\widehat{Y}is the Liouville vector field ofλ^\hat{\lambda}. ThenφH,G∗​αG=efH,G​αH\varphi_{H,G}^{*}\alpha_{G}=e^{f_{H,G}}\alpha_{H}. Under the natural identification induced byφH,G\varphi_{H,G}, the domainWφH,G∗​(αG)W^{\varphi_{H,G}^{*}(\alpha_{G})}is Liouville isomorphic toWGW_{G}. Apply (28), we havedSBM​(WαH,WφH,G∗​(αG))≤dCBM​(αH,φH,G∗​αG)=dCBM​(αH,αG).d_{\rm SBM}(W^{\alpha_{H}},W^{\varphi_{H,G}^{*}(\alpha_{G})})\leq d_{\rm CBM}(\alpha_{H},\varphi^{*}_{H,G}\alpha_{G})=d_{\rm CBM}(\alpha_{H},\alpha_{G}).

Then we will show that for anyH,G∈ℋ+​(W,λ)H,G\in\mathcal{H}^{+}(W,\lambda), we havedCBM​(αH,αG)≤dT​(H,G).d_{\rm CBM}(\alpha_{H},\alpha_{G})\leq d_{\rm T}(H,G).

Indeed, if1C​WG⊂WH\frac{1}{C}W_{G}\subset W_{H}, thenfH,G≤ln⁡Cf_{H,G}\leq\ln C. Similarly, if1C​WH⊂WG\frac{1}{C}W_{H}\subset W_{G}, thenfG,H≤ln⁡Cf_{G,H}\leq\ln C, i.e.fH,G≥−ln⁡Cf_{H,G}\geq-\ln C.
By Proposition3.2, if1C​G≤H≤C​G\frac{1}{C}G\leq H\leq CG, then1C​WG⊂WH\frac{1}{C}W_{G}\subset W_{H}and1C​WH⊂WG\frac{1}{C}W_{H}\subset W_{G}. Subsequently, we have|fH,G|≤ln⁡C|f_{H,G}|\leq\ln C, which implies thatdCBM​(αH,αG)≤ln⁡C.d_{\rm CBM}(\alpha_{H},\alpha_{G})\leq\ln C.Then for anyH,G∈ℋ+​(W,λ)H,G\in\mathcal{H}^{+}(W,\lambda), we havedCBM​(αH,αG)≤inf{ln⁡C≥0|1C​H≤G≤C​H}.d_{\rm CBM}(\alpha_{H},\alpha_{G})\leq\inf\left\{\ln C\geq 0\,\left|\,\frac{1}{C}H\leq G\leq CH\right\}\right..

By Lemma2.5, we have(WG,λ^)≅(WG∘φ,λ^)(W_{G},\hat{\lambda})\cong(W_{G\circ\varphi},\hat{\lambda})for anyφ∈Symp​(W,λ)\varphi\in{\rm Symp}(W,\lambda), which implies that(∂WG,αG)≅(∂WG∘φ,αG∘φ)(\partial W_{G},\alpha_{G})\cong(\partial W_{G\circ\varphi},\alpha_{G\circ\varphi}). Then we havedCBM​(αH,αG)≤inf{ln⁡C|∃φ∈Symp​(W,λ),s.t.​1C​H≤G∘φ≤C​H}=dT​(H,G).d_{\rm CBM}(\alpha_{H},\alpha_{G})\leq\inf\left\{\ln C\,\left|\,\exists\,\varphi\in{\rm Symp}(W,\lambda),\text{ s.t. }\frac{1}{C}H\leq G\circ\varphi\leq CH\right\}\right.=d_{\rm T}(H,G).

## 6.Proof of TheoremD

The following proposition relates the entropies of two flows that differ by a time reparameterization.

## Proposition 6.1.

Let(X,d)(X,d)be a compact metric space, and letφ={φt}t∈ℝ\varphi=\{\varphi_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{R}}andψ={ψt}t∈ℝ\psi=\{\psi_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{R}}be continuous flows onXXwithout fixed points.
Assume thatψt\psi_{t}is a time reparameterization ofφt\varphi_{t}, namely there exists a continuous mapτ:X×ℝ→ℝ\tau:X\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}such that for everyx∈Xx\in X, the mapt↦τ​(x,t)t\mapsto\tau(x,t)is strictly increasing, surjective, andψt​(x)=φτ​(x,t)​(x)\psi_{t}(x)=\varphi_{\tau(x,t)}(x), for allx∈Xx\in Xandt∈ℝ.t\in\mathbb{R}.Suppose moreover that there exist constants0<c≤C0<c\leq Csuch that for allx∈Xx\in Xandt≥0t\geq 0,c​t≤τ​(x,t)≤C​tct\leq\tau(x,t)\leq Ct. Thenc​htop​(φt)≤htop​(ψt)≤C​htop​(φt).c\,h_{\mathrm{top}}(\varphi_{t})\leq h_{\mathrm{top}}(\psi_{t})\leq C\,h_{\mathrm{top}}(\varphi_{t}).

## Proof.

This is a special case of Ohno’s theorem on weakly equivalent flows without fixed points; see Theorem 1 in[32]. In the present situation, the boundsc​t≤τ​(x,t)≤C​tct\leq\tau(x,t)\leq Ctimply that the additive functional defining the time change has time-one value bounded from below byccand from above byCC. Thereforec​htop​(φ1)≤htop​(ψ1)≤C​htop​(φ1)c\,h_{\rm top}(\varphi_{1})\leq h_{\rm top}(\psi_{1})\leq C\,h_{\rm top}(\varphi_{1}).
∎

Before proving TheoremD, let us recall the definition of the topological entropy of a flow on some subset. ForT>0T>0andε>0\varepsilon>0, denote byNU​(T,ε)N_{U}(T,\varepsilon)the maximal cardinality of a(T,ε)(T,\varepsilon)-separated set ofφ\varphiinUU.
The topological entropy ofφt\varphi^{t}onUUis defined ashtop​(φ,U)=limε→0lim supT→∞1T​ln⁡NU​(T,ε).h_{\mathrm{top}}(\varphi,U)=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\limsup_{T\to\infty}\frac{1}{T}\ln N_{U}(T,\varepsilon).

IfU1⊂U2U_{1}\subset U_{2}, then clearlyhtop​(φ,U1)≤htop​(φ,U2)h_{\mathrm{top}}(\varphi,U_{1})\leq h_{\mathrm{top}}(\varphi,U_{2}).

## Proposition 6.2.

IfUUis a dense subset ofXX, then we havehtop​(φ,U)=htop​(φ,X).h_{\rm top}(\varphi,U)=h_{\rm top}(\varphi,X).

## Proof.

SinceU⊂XU\subset X, we havehtop​(φ,U)≤htop​(φ)h_{\rm top}(\varphi,U)\leq h_{\rm top}(\varphi).

For the reverse inequality, fixε>0\varepsilon>0. Becauseφt\varphi^{t}is uniformly continuous on the compact space[0,T]×X[0,T]\times X, there existsδ>0\delta>0such that ifd​(x,y)<δd(x,y)<\delta, thensup0≤t≤Td​(φt​(x),φt​(y))<ε4\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}d(\varphi^{t}(x),\varphi^{t}(y))<\frac{\varepsilon}{4}. LetE⊂XE\subset Xbe a maximal(T,ε)(T,\varepsilon)-separated set inXX(so|E|=NX​(T,ε)|E|=N_{X}(T,\varepsilon)). SinceUUis dense, for eachx∈Ex\in Ewe can choose a pointx′∈Ux^{\prime}\in Uwithd​(x,x′)<δd(x,x^{\prime})<\delta. SetE′={x′:x∈E}⊂UE^{\prime}=\{x^{\prime}:x\in E\}\subset U. We claim thatE′E^{\prime}is(T,ε/2)(T,\varepsilon/2)-separated. Indeed, take two distinct pointsx′,y′∈E′x^{\prime},y^{\prime}\in E^{\prime}with correspondingx,y∈Ex,y\in E. BecauseEEis(T,ε)(T,\varepsilon)-separated, there existst∈[0,T]t\in[0,T]such thatd​(φt​(x),φt​(y))≥εd(\varphi^{t}(x),\varphi^{t}(y))\geq\varepsilon. Then, using the choice ofδ\delta,d​(φt​(x′),φt​(y′))\displaystyle d(\varphi^{t}(x^{\prime}),\varphi^{t}(y^{\prime}))≥d​(φt​(x),φt​(y))−d​(φt​(x),φt​(x′))−d​(φt​(y),φt​(y′))\displaystyle\geq d(\varphi^{t}(x),\varphi^{t}(y))-d(\varphi^{t}(x),\varphi^{t}(x^{\prime}))-d(\varphi^{t}(y),\varphi^{t}(y^{\prime}))>ε−ε4−ε4=ε2.\displaystyle>\varepsilon-\frac{\varepsilon}{4}-\frac{\varepsilon}{4}=\frac{\varepsilon}{2}.

ThusE′E^{\prime}is(T,ε/2)(T,\varepsilon/2)-separated and|E′|=|E||E^{\prime}|=|E|. Consequently,NU​(T,ε/2)≥NX​(T,ε)N_{U}(T,\varepsilon/2)\geq N_{X}(T,\varepsilon),
which implies thathtop​(φ,U)≥htop​(φ)h_{\rm top}(\varphi,U)\geq h_{\mathrm{top}}(\varphi).
∎

In the relative situation, one considers a factor mapπ\pibetween a continuous dynamical systemT:X→XT\colon X\rightarrow Xand a second systemS:Y→YS\colon Y\rightarrow Y. That is,π:X→Y\pi\colon X\rightarrow Yis continuous and onto and satisfiesπ∘T=S∘π\pi\circ T=S\circ\pi. Then we have the following result.

## Theorem 6.3(Theorem 17 in[7], Proposition 3.1.6 in[24]).

Let(X,d)(X,d)and(Y,d′)(Y,d^{\prime})be compact metric spaces andT:X→XT\colon X\rightarrow XandS:Y→YS\colon Y\rightarrow Ybe continuous. Letπ:X→Y\pi\colon X\rightarrow Ybe a continuous factor map. Thenhtop​(S)≤htop​(T)≤htop​(S)+supy∈Yhtop​(T,π−1​y).h_{\rm top}(S)\leq h_{\rm top}(T)\leq h_{\rm top}(S)+\sup_{y\in Y}h_{\rm top}(T,\pi^{-1}y).

Now, let us give the proof of TheoremD.

## Proof of TheoremD.

We fix some notations first. Setτmin≔minS1×W⁡τλ,Handτmax≔maxS1×W⁡τλ,H.\tau_{\min}\coloneqq\min_{S^{1}\times W}\tau_{\lambda,H}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\tau_{\max}\coloneqq\max_{S^{1}\times W}\tau_{\lambda,H}.

DenoteX≔S1×WX\coloneqq S^{1}\times W,X∘≔S1×Int​(W)X^{\circ}\coloneqq S^{1}\times{\rm Int}(W), andM∘≔∂WH∖(∂W×{0})M^{\circ}\coloneqq\partial W_{H}\setminus(\partial W\times\{0\}).

Adding and subtractinges​H​(t,ℒλ−s​(w))e^{s}H(t,\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}^{-s}(w))inside the integral gives‖A−H‖C0≤eρ​‖F−H‖C0+sup0≤s≤ρ‖es​H​(t,ℒλ−s​(w))−H​(t,w)‖C0<η.\|A-H\|_{C^{0}}\leq e^{\rho}\|F-H\|_{C^{0}}+\sup_{0\leq s\leq\rho}\left\|e^{s}H(t,\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}^{-s}(w))-H(t,w)\right\|_{C^{0}}<\eta.

If(r,x)∈[−ρ,0]×∂W(r,x)\in[-\rho,0]\times\partial Wand0≤s≤ρ0\leq s\leq\rho, thenℒλ−s​(r,x)=(r−s,x)∈[−2​ρ,0]×∂W\mathscr{L}_{\lambda}^{-s}(r,x)=(r-s,x)\in[-2\rho,0]\times\partial W. Using‖F−H‖C0<δ\|F-H\|_{C^{0}}<\delta, we obtain‖A|[−ρ,0]×∂W∥C0≤eρ​(‖H‖C0​(S1×[−2​ρ,0]×∂W)+δ)<η.\|A|_{[-\rho,0]\times\partial W}\|_{C^{0}}\leq e^{\rho}\left(\|H\|_{C^{0}(S^{1}\times[-2\rho,0]\times\partial W)}+\delta\right)<\eta.

ThusAAhas all the required properties.
∎

Before we prove Lemma7.4, we need the following lemma.

## Lemma 7.5.

LetΣ\Sigmabe a compact manifold and letu:S1×[−ρ,0]×Σ→(0,∞)u:S^{1}\times[-\rho,0]\times\Sigma\to(0,\infty)be smooth with∂ru<0\partial_{r}u<0. Then there exists a smooth functionu~:S1×[−ρ,0]×Σ→[0,∞)\widetilde{u}:S^{1}\times[-\rho,0]\times\Sigma\to[0,\infty)such that:
- (1)

u~=u\widetilde{u}=unearr=−ρr=-\rho;
- (2)

u~​(t,0,x)=0\widetilde{u}(t,0,x)=0andu~​(t,r,x)>0\widetilde{u}(t,r,x)>0forr<0r<0;
- (3)

∂ru~<0\partial_{r}\widetilde{u}<0;
- (4)

u~\widetilde{u}is independent ofttnearr=0r=0;
- (5)

0≤u~​(t,r,x)≤max(t,x)∈S1×Σ⁡u​(t,−ρ,x)0\leq\widetilde{u}(t,r,x)\leq\max_{(t,x)\in S^{1}\times\Sigma}u(t,-\rho,x).

## Proof.

Sinceu>0u>0andS1×ΣS^{1}\times\Sigmais compact, the functionu​(t,−ρ,x)u(t,-\rho,x)has a uniform positive lower bound. We first choose a smooth positive functionp0p_{0}onS1×[−ρ,0]×ΣS^{1}\times[-\rho,0]\times\Sigmasuch thatp0=−∂rup_{0}=-\partial_{r}unearr=−ρr=-\rho,p0p_{0}is independent ofttnearr=0r=0, and∫−ρ0p0​(t,s,x)​𝑑s<u​(t,−ρ,x)\int_{-\rho}^{0}p_{0}(t,s,x)\,ds<u(t,-\rho,x)

for every(t,x)(t,x).

To see that this is possible, retain−∂ru-\partial_{r}uonly on a sufficiently small neighborhood ofr=−ρr=-\rho, choose a sufficiently small positivett-independent function nearr=0r=0, and join the two functions smoothly and positively across the middle of the collar. By making the two end neighborhoods and the intermediate function sufficiently small, the total integral ofp0p_{0}can be made uniformly smaller thanu​(t,−ρ,x)u(t,-\rho,x).

Choose a fixed nonnegative smooth functionβ\beta, supported in the interior of(−ρ,0)(-\rho,0), such that∫−ρ0β​(s)​𝑑s=1\int_{-\rho}^{0}\beta(s)\,ds=1. Then definep​(t,r,x)≔p0​(t,r,x)+(u​(t,−ρ,x)−∫−ρ0p0​(t,s,x)​𝑑s)​β​(r).p(t,r,x)\coloneqq p_{0}(t,r,x)+\left(u(t,-\rho,x)-\int_{-\rho}^{0}p_{0}(t,s,x)\,ds\right)\beta(r).

Sinceβ\betais supported away from the two ends of the collar, the functionppstill satisfiesp=−∂rup=-\partial_{r}unearr=−ρr=-\rhoand is independent ofttnearr=0r=0. Moreover,p>0p>0and∫−ρ0p​(t,s,x)​𝑑s=u​(t,−ρ,x)\int_{-\rho}^{0}p(t,s,x)\,ds=u(t,-\rho,x).

Now defineu~​(t,r,x)≔∫r0p​(t,s,x)​𝑑s\widetilde{u}(t,r,x)\coloneqq\int_{r}^{0}p(t,s,x)\,ds. Becausep>0p>0, the functionu~\widetilde{u}is strictly positive forr<0r<0, vanishes atr=0r=0, and satisfies∂ru~=−p<0\partial_{r}\widetilde{u}=-p<0. At the inner end of the collar, the integral identity givesu~​(t,−ρ,x)=u​(t,−ρ,x)\widetilde{u}(t,-\rho,x)=u(t,-\rho,x). Sincep=−∂rup=-\partial_{r}unearr=−ρr=-\rho, we also have∂ru~=∂ru\partial_{r}\widetilde{u}=\partial_{r}uthere. So,u~=u\widetilde{u}=unearr=−ρr=-\rho. Nearr=0r=0, the functionppis independent oftt, and therefore so isu~\widetilde{u}. Finally, positivity ofppgives0≤u~​(t,r,x)≤∫−ρ0p​(t,s,x)​𝑑s=u​(t,−ρ,x)0\leq\widetilde{u}(t,r,x)\leq\int_{-\rho}^{0}p(t,s,x)\,ds=u(t,-\rho,x), which proves the required estimate.
∎

Write the fixed Liouville collar as[−ρ,0]×∂W[-\rho,0]\times\partial W, whereYλ=∂rY_{\lambda}=\partial_{r}. Defineu​(t,r,x)≔e−r​A​(t,r,x)u(t,r,x)\coloneqq e^{-r}A(t,r,x). ForH,G∈ℋ0​(W,λ)H,G\in\mathcal{H}^{0}(W,\lambda), defineδλ​(H,G)≔inf{ε≥0|ℒλ^−ε​(WH)⊂WG​and​ℒλ^−ε​(WG)⊂WH}.\delta_{\lambda}(H,G)\coloneqq\inf\Bigl\{\varepsilon\geq 0\,\Bigm|\,\mathscr{L}_{\hat{\lambda}}^{-\varepsilon}(W_{H})\subset W_{G}\text{ and }\mathscr{L}_{\hat{\lambda}}^{-\varepsilon}(W_{G})\subset W_{H}\Bigr\}.

We now define the barcode forH∈ℋ0​(W,λ)H\in\mathcal{H}^{0}(W,\lambda). Choose smooth HamiltoniansHk∈ℋ​(W,λ)H_{k}\in\mathcal{H}(W,\lambda)withHk→HH_{k}\to HinC0C^{0}. Then it follows from the triangle inequality forδλ\delta_{\lambda}that, for all sufficiently largek,ℓk,\ell,δλ​(Hk,Hℓ)≤δλ​(Hk,H)+δλ​(H,Hℓ)≤ε\delta_{\lambda}(H_{k},H_{\ell})\leq\delta_{\lambda}(H_{k},H)+\delta_{\lambda}(H,H_{\ell})\leq\varepsilon. Applying (29) to the smooth pair(Hk,Hℓ)(H_{k},H_{\ell}), we getdbot​(ℬ​(Hk),ℬ​(Hℓ))≤ε.d_{\rm bot}(\mathcal{B}(H_{k}),\mathcal{B}(H_{\ell}))\leq\varepsilon.

Hence{ℬ​(Hk)}\{\mathcal{B}(H_{k})\}is Cauchy, and we defineℬ​(H)≔limk→∞ℬ​(Hk)\mathcal{B}(H)\coloneqq\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{B}(H_{k}).

Note that this definition does not depend on the chosen smooth approximation. Indeed, ifGk∈ℋ​(W,λ)G_{k}\in\mathcal{H}(W,\lambda)is another sequence withGk→HG_{k}\to HinC0C^{0}, then the previous paragraph givesδλ​(Hk,H)≤ε/2\delta_{\lambda}(H_{k},H)\leq\varepsilon/2andδλ​(Gk,H)≤ε/2\delta_{\lambda}(G_{k},H)\leq\varepsilon/2for all largekk. Then, by triangle inequalities ofdSBMd_{\rm SBM}anddbotd_{\rm bot}, we havedbot​(ℬ​(H),ℬ​(G))\displaystyle d_{\rm bot}(\mathcal{B}(H),\mathcal{B}(G))≤dbot​(ℬ​(H),ℬ​(Hη))+dbot​(ℬ​(Hη),ℬ​(Gη))+dbot​(ℬ​(Gη),ℬ​(G))\displaystyle\leq d_{\rm bot}(\mathcal{B}(H),\mathcal{B}(H_{\eta}))+d_{\rm bot}(\mathcal{B}(H_{\eta}),\mathcal{B}(G_{\eta}))+d_{\rm bot}(\mathcal{B}(G_{\eta}),\mathcal{B}(G))≤dSBM​(WHη,WGη)+2​η\displaystyle\leq d_{\rm SBM}(W_{H_{\eta}},W_{G_{\eta}})+2\eta≤dSBM​(WHη,WH)+dSBM​(WH,WG)+dSBM​(WGη,WG)\displaystyle\leq d_{\rm SBM}(W_{H_{\eta}},W_{H})+d_{\rm SBM}(W_{H},W_{G})+d_{\rm SBM}(W_{G_{\eta}},W_{G})≤dSBM​(WH,WG)+4​η.\displaystyle\leq d_{\rm SBM}(W_{H},W_{G})+4\eta.

Here, the second inequality comes from (31). Lettingη→0\eta\to 0proves the desired inequalitydbot​(ℬ​(H),ℬ​(G))≤dSBM​(WH,WG)d_{\rm bot}(\mathcal{B}(H),\mathcal{B}(G))\leq d_{\rm SBM}(W_{H},W_{G})forH,G∈ℋ0​(W,λ)H,G\in\mathcal{H}^{0}(W,\lambda).

for any fixedψ∈Symp​(W,λ)\psi\in\mathrm{Symp}(W,\lambda). Then we claim suchK+(p),K−(p)∈ℋ​(W,λ)K_{+}^{(p)},K_{-}^{(p)}\in\mathcal{H}(W,\lambda).

Then for theτ\taufunction in (3), we haveτλ,F​(f,g)\displaystyle\tau_{\lambda,F(f,g)}=ιXF​(f,g)​λ+F​(f,g)\displaystyle=\iota_{X_{F(f,g)}}\lambda+F(f,g)=(∂1F)​(f,g)​ιXf​λ+(∂2F)​(f,g)​ιXg​λ+F​(f,g)\displaystyle=(\partial_{1}F)(f,g)\,\iota_{X_{f}}\lambda+(\partial_{2}F)(f,g)\,\iota_{X_{g}}\lambda+F(f,g)=(∂1F)​(f,g)​ιXf​λ+(∂2F)​(f,g)​ιXg​λ+(∂1F)​(f,g)​f+(∂2F)​(f,g)​g\displaystyle=(\partial_{1}F)(f,g)\,\iota_{X_{f}}\lambda+(\partial_{2}F)(f,g)\,\iota_{X_{g}}\lambda+(\partial_{1}F)(f,g)f+(\partial_{2}F)(f,g)g=(∂1F)​(f,g)​(ιXf​λ+f)+(∂2F)​(f,g)​(ιXg​λ+g)\displaystyle=(\partial_{1}F)(f,g)(\iota_{X_{f}}\lambda+f)+(\partial_{2}F)(f,g)(\iota_{X_{g}}\lambda+g)=(∂1F)​(f,g)​τλ,f+(∂2F)​(f,g)​τλ,g\displaystyle=(\partial_{1}F)(f,g)\tau_{\lambda,f}+(\partial_{2}F)(f,g)\tau_{\lambda,g}

where the third equality uses Euler’s identity for positively homogeneous functions of degree11. Since∂1F,∂2F≥0\partial_{1}F,\partial_{2}F\geq 0,f,g∈ℋ​(W,λ)f,g\in\mathcal{H}(W,\lambda)(hence,τλ,f>0\tau_{\lambda,f}>0andτλ,g>0\tau_{\lambda,g}>0)
implies thatτλ,F​(f,g)>0\tau_{\lambda,F(f,g)}>0. Applying this to the functionF​(a,b)=(ap+bp)1/pF(a,b)=(a^{p}+b^{p})^{1/p}andF​(a,b)=(a−p+b−p)−1/pF(a,b)=(a^{-p}+b^{-p})^{-1/p}respectively, we get the desired claim thatK+(p),K−(p)∈ℋ​(W,λ)K_{+}^{(p)},K_{-}^{(p)}\in\mathcal{H}(W,\lambda).

Next, by the elementary inequalitiesmax⁡(a,b)≤(ap+bp)1/p≤21/p​max⁡(a,b),\max(a,b)\leq(a^{p}+b^{p})^{1/p}\leq 2^{1/p}\max(a,b),

and2−1/p​min⁡(a,b)≤(a−p+b−p)−1/p≤min⁡(a,b),2^{-1/p}\min(a,b)\leq(a^{-p}+b^{-p})^{-1/p}\leq\min(a,b),

we obtainK−(p)≤min⁡{H,G∘ψ}≤max⁡{H,G∘ψ}≤K+(p)K_{-}^{(p)}\leq\min\{H,G\circ\psi\}\leq\max\{H,G\circ\psi\}\leq K_{+}^{(p)}. Therefore,K−(p)K_{-}^{(p)}andK+(p)K_{+}^{(p)}satisfy the condition on the right-hand side of (19). Moreover, the inequalities above also imply thatℓ​(WK−(p),K+(p))\displaystyle\ell(W_{K_{-}^{(p)},K_{+}^{(p)}})=ln⁡(sup(t,w)∈S1×Int​(W)K+(p)​(t,w)K−(p)​(t,w))\displaystyle=\ln\left(\sup_{(t,w)\in S^{1}\times{\rm Int}(W)}\frac{K_{+}^{(p)}(t,w)}{K_{-}^{(p)}(t,w)}\right)≤ln⁡(sup(t,w)∈S1×Int​(W)max⁡{H​(t,w),G​(t,ψ​(w))}min⁡{H​(t,w),G​(t,ψ​(w))})+2​ln⁡2p\displaystyle\leq\ln\left(\sup_{(t,w)\in S^{1}\times{\rm Int}(W)}\frac{\max\{H(t,w),G(t,\psi(w))\}}{\min\{H(t,w),G(t,\psi(w))\}}\right)+\frac{2\ln 2}{p}=ln⁡(sup(t,w)∈S1×Int​(W)max⁡{H​(t,w)G​(t,ψ​(w)),G​(t,ψ​(w))H​(t,w)})+2​ln⁡2p.\displaystyle=\ln\left(\sup_{(t,w)\in S^{1}\times{\rm Int}(W)}\max\left\{\frac{H(t,w)}{G(t,\psi(w))},\frac{G(t,\psi(w))}{H(t,w)}\right\}\right)+\frac{2\ln 2}{p}.

Then we will give the proof of TheoremH.

## Proof of TheoremH.

We first verify the two axioms in the statement. For (i), takingψ=id\psi={\rm id}in(20)(\ref{hom-tun-cat})giveshomTun​(W,λ)​(H,H)≤max⁡{ln⁡(sup(t,w)∈S1×Int​(W)H​(t,w)/H​(t,w)),0}=0.{\rm hom}_{{\rm Tun}(W,\lambda)}(H,H)\leq\max\left\{\ln(\sup_{(t,w)\in S^{1}\times{\rm Int}(W)}H(t,w)/H(t,w)),0\right\}=0.

Since the right-hand side of(20)(\ref{hom-tun-cat})is always nonnegative, we obtainhomTun​(W,λ)​(H,H)=0{\rm hom}_{{\rm Tun}(W,\lambda)}(H,H)=0.

For (ii), letH1,H2,H3∈ℋ​(W,λ)H_{1},H_{2},H_{3}\in\mathcal{H}(W,\lambda), and fixϵ>0\epsilon>0. If eitherhomTun​(W,λ)​(H1,H2){\rm hom}_{{\rm Tun}(W,\lambda)}(H_{1},H_{2})orhomTun​(W,λ)​(H2,H3){\rm hom}_{{\rm Tun}(W,\lambda)}(H_{2},H_{3})is infinite, then the desired inequality is immediate. Thus we may assume that both quantities are finite.

By the definition of the infimum, there existψ,φ∈Symp​(W,λ)\psi,\varphi\in{\rm Symp}(W,\lambda)such that, withAψ≔sup(t,w)∈S1×Int​(W)H1​(t,w)/H2​(t,ψ​(w))A_{\psi}\coloneqq\sup_{(t,w)\in S^{1}\times{\rm Int}(W)}H_{1}(t,w)/H_{2}(t,\psi(w))andBφ≔sup(t,w)∈S1×Int​(W)H2​(t,w)/H3​(t,φ​(w))B_{\varphi}\coloneqq\sup_{(t,w)\in S^{1}\times{\rm Int}(W)}H_{2}(t,w)/H_{3}(t,\varphi(w)), we havemax⁡{ln⁡Aψ,0}≤homTun​(W,λ)​(H1,H2)+ϵ,max⁡{ln⁡Bφ,0}≤homTun​(W,λ)​(H2,H3)+ϵ.\max\{\ln A_{\psi},0\}\leq{\rm hom}_{{\rm Tun}(W,\lambda)}(H_{1},H_{2})+\epsilon,\,\,\,\max\{\ln B_{\varphi},0\}\leq{\rm hom}_{{\rm Tun}(W,\lambda)}(H_{2},H_{3})+\epsilon.

For every(t,w)∈S1×Int​(W)(t,w)\in S^{1}\times{\rm Int}(W), we haveH1​(t,w)H3​(t,φ​(ψ​(w)))=H1​(t,w)H2​(t,ψ​(w))⋅H2​(t,ψ​(w))H3​(t,φ​(ψ​(w))).\frac{H_{1}(t,w)}{H_{3}(t,\varphi(\psi(w)))}=\frac{H_{1}(t,w)}{H_{2}(t,\psi(w))}\cdot\frac{H_{2}(t,\psi(w))}{H_{3}(t,\varphi(\psi(w)))}.

Taking the supremum and using thatψ\psimapsInt​(W){\rm Int}(W)diffeomorphically onto itself, we getsup(t,w)∈S1×Int​(W)H1​(t,w)H3​(t,φ​(ψ​(w)))≤Aψ​Bφ.\sup_{(t,w)\in S^{1}\times{\rm Int}(W)}\frac{H_{1}(t,w)}{H_{3}(t,\varphi(\psi(w)))}\leq A_{\psi}B_{\varphi}.

Therefore,homTun​(W,λ)​(H1,H3)≤max⁡{ln⁡(Aψ​Bφ),0}≤max⁡{ln⁡Aψ,0}+max⁡{ln⁡Bφ,0}{\rm hom}_{{\rm Tun}(W,\lambda)}(H_{1},H_{3})\leq\max\{\ln(A_{\psi}B_{\varphi}),0\}\leq\max\{\ln A_{\psi},0\}+\max\{\ln B_{\varphi},0\}. Combining the last inequality with the choices ofψ\psiandφ\varphi, we obtainhomTun​(W,λ)​(H1,H3)≤homTun​(W,λ)​(H1,H2)+homTun​(W,λ)​(H2,H3)+2​ϵ.{\rm hom}_{{\rm Tun}(W,\lambda)}(H_{1},H_{3})\leq{\rm hom}_{{\rm Tun}(W,\lambda)}(H_{1},H_{2})+{\rm hom}_{{\rm Tun}(W,\lambda)}(H_{2},H_{3})+2\epsilon.

Lettingϵ→0\epsilon\to 0proves the triangle inequality.

It remains to identify the symmetrization with the Thompson-type metric. For fixedψ∈Symp​(W,λ)\psi\in{\rm Symp}(W,\lambda), the condition(1/C)​H​(t,w)≤G​(t,ψ​(w))≤C​H​(t,w)(1/C)H(t,w)\leq G(t,\psi(w))\leq CH(t,w)for all(t,w)∈S1×Int​(W)(t,w)\in S^{1}\times{\rm Int}(W)is equivalent tomax⁡{H​(t,w)G​(t,ψ​(w)),G​(t,ψ​(w))H​(t,w)}≤C\max\left\{\frac{H(t,w)}{G(t,\psi(w))},\frac{G(t,\psi(w))}{H(t,w)}\right\}\leq C

for all(t,w)∈S1×Int​(W)(t,w)\in S^{1}\times{\rm Int}(W). Hence, for this fixedψ\psi, the smallest possible value ofln⁡C\ln Cisln⁡(sup(t,w)∈S1×Int​(W)max⁡{H​(t,w)G​(t,ψ​(w)),G​(t,ψ​(w))H​(t,w)}).\ln\left(\sup_{(t,w)\in S^{1}\times{\rm Int}(W)}\max\left\{\frac{H(t,w)}{G(t,\psi(w))},\frac{G(t,\psi(w))}{H(t,w)}\right\}\right).

Taking the infimum over allψ∈Symp​(W,λ)\psi\in{\rm Symp}(W,\lambda), and comparing with Definition1.3, giveshomTun​(W,λ)sym​(H,G)=dT​(H,G){\rm hom}_{{\rm Tun}(W,\lambda)}^{\rm sym}(H,G)=d_{\rm T}(H,G). This completes the proof.
∎

## 11.Proof of Lemma1.38

We first recall the standard positivity-of-intersection input that will be used below. By assumption,DDisJJ-holomorphic andXKNX_{K_{N}}is tangent toDDalongDD, the Floer equation is compatible with the divisorDD. Concretely, near any point ofDD, choose local complex coordinates(x,z)(x,z), whereD={z=0}D=\{z=0\}. The assumptionJ​T​D=T​DJTD=TDsays that the normal direction toDDisJJ-complex, while the conditionXKN|D⊂T​DX_{K_{N}}|_{D}\subset TDimplies that the normal component of the Hamiltonian perturbation vanishes alongDD. Therefore, the normal componentuℂu_{\mathbb{C}}ofuusatisfies a Cauchy–Riemann type equation with lower-order terms. By the similarity principle, the zeros ofuℂu_{\mathbb{C}}are isolated and have positive order, unlessuℂ≡0u_{\mathbb{C}}\equiv 0on a connected component. See, for example, Appendix E in[29]for positivity of intersections and Lemma 7.1 in[9]for the corresponding divisor argument in the presence of compatible perturbations.

In our situation,uucannot be entirely contained inDD. Indeed,DDis preserved by the Hamiltonian flow ofKNK_{N}, so a Hamiltonian orbit which meetsDDis contained inDD. Since neitherγ−\gamma_{-}norγ+\gamma_{+}is contained inDD, both asymptotic orbits are disjoint fromDD. By the exponential convergence of finite-energy Floer cylinders to their asymptotic orbits,uuis also disjoint fromDDfor|s||s|sufficiently large. Thereforeu−1​(D)u^{-1}(D)is a finite set, and the intersection number is given byu⋅D=∑p∈u−1​(D)multp​(u,D)u\cdot D=\sum_{p\in u^{-1}(D)}{\rm mult}_{p}(u,D). Each local multiplicity is positive, sou⋅D≥0u\cdot D\geq 0, andu⋅D=0u\cdot D=0if and only ifu−1​(D)=∅u^{-1}(D)=\varnothing, equivalentlyu∩D=∅u\cap D=\varnothing.

It remains to identify this intersection number with the change of winding number. Letuℂu_{\mathbb{C}}denote thezz-component ofuu. On the complement ofu−1​(D)u^{-1}(D), the one-formd​θℂ=d​arg⁡zd\theta_{\mathbb{C}}=d\arg zis defined. ChooseR>0R>0large enough so thatuuis disjoint fromDDon((−∞,−R]∪[R,∞))×S1((-\infty,-R]\cup[R,\infty))\times S^{1}, and choose small pairwise disjoint disksBϵ​(p)B_{\epsilon}(p)around the finitely many pointsp∈u−1​(D)∩([−R,R]×S1)p\in u^{-1}(D)\cap([-R,R]\times S^{1}). SetΣR,ϵ≔[−R,R]×S1∖⋃pBϵ​(p)\Sigma_{R,\epsilon}\coloneqq[-R,R]\times S^{1}\setminus\bigcup_{p}B_{\epsilon}(p). Sinced​(d​θℂ)=0d(d\theta_{\mathbb{C}})=0onℂ∗\mathbb{C}^{*}, Stokes’ theorem gives0=∫∂ΣR,ϵuℂ∗​𝑑θℂ=∫{R}×S1uℂ∗​𝑑θℂ−∫{−R}×S1uℂ∗​𝑑θℂ−∑p∈u−1​(D)∫∂Bϵ​(p)uℂ∗​𝑑θℂ.0=\int_{\partial\Sigma_{R,\epsilon}}u_{\mathbb{C}}^{*}d\theta_{\mathbb{C}}=\int_{\{R\}\times S^{1}}u_{\mathbb{C}}^{*}d\theta_{\mathbb{C}}-\int_{\{-R\}\times S^{1}}u_{\mathbb{C}}^{*}d\theta_{\mathbb{C}}-\sum_{p\in u^{-1}(D)}\int_{\partial B_{\epsilon}(p)}u_{\mathbb{C}}^{*}d\theta_{\mathbb{C}}.

With the boundary orientation induced fromΣR,ϵ\Sigma_{R,\epsilon}, the circles∂Bϵ​(p)\partial B_{\epsilon}(p)are negatively oriented. Thus(2​π)−1​∫∂Bϵ​(p)uℂ∗​𝑑θℂ=ordp​(uℂ)(2\pi)^{-1}\int_{\partial B_{\epsilon}(p)}u_{\mathbb{C}}^{*}d\theta_{\mathbb{C}}={\rm ord}_{p}(u_{\mathbb{C}}), whereordp​(uℂ){\rm ord}_{p}(u_{\mathbb{C}})is the vanishing order of the normal component atpp. Therefore,∑p∈u−1​(D)ordp​(uℂ)=12​π​∫{R}×S1uℂ∗​𝑑θℂ−12​π​∫{−R}×S1uℂ∗​𝑑θℂ.\sum_{p\in u^{-1}(D)}{\rm ord}_{p}(u_{\mathbb{C}})=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\{R\}\times S^{1}}u_{\mathbb{C}}^{*}d\theta_{\mathbb{C}}-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\{-R\}\times S^{1}}u_{\mathbb{C}}^{*}d\theta_{\mathbb{C}}.

LettingR→∞R\to\infty, the exponential convergence ofuutoγ+\gamma_{+}at+∞+\inftyand toγ−\gamma_{-}at−∞-\inftyimplies that the two boundary integrals converge to the winding numbersk​(γ+)k(\gamma_{+})andk​(γ−)k(\gamma_{-}), respectively. Since the local intersection multiplicity withD={z=0}D=\{z=0\}is exactly the vanishing order ofuℂu_{\mathbb{C}}, the left-hand side isu⋅Du\cdot D. Therefore,u⋅D=k​(γ+)−k​(γ−)u\cdot D=k(\gamma_{+})-k(\gamma_{-}).

Sinceu⋅D≥0u\cdot D\geq 0, we obtaink​(γ+)≥k​(γ−)k(\gamma_{+})\geq k(\gamma_{-}). Thus, with the convention in this lemma that a Floer cylinder contributing to the differential goes from the negative endγ−\gamma_{-}to the positive endγ+\gamma_{+}, the Floer differential cannot decrease the winding number.
